<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now write app.ts edits:

1. imports: add to value import: `budgetOf, difficultyOf, difficultyViews, DIFFICULTY_LABEL, DEFAULT_DIFFICULTY, isDifficulty`? We need: budgetOf, difficultyOf, difficultyViews. Add type Difficulty.
2. panelFrom takes difficulty.
3. health adds difficulties.
4. /api/contracts: `const diff = difficultyOf(c.req.query('difficulty'));` → panelFrom(ids, diff), budget: budgetOf(diff).
5. createSession: parse `const difficulty = difficultyOf(body['difficulty']);` (but echo the raw? use difficultyOf directly), resolved = resolvePanel(db, panel, budgetOf(difficulty)), insertSession budget config = budgetOf(difficulty), meta difficulty, response difficulty.
6. run route reads meta (fine).

Note `difficultyOf` returns Difficulty; if body has invalid value it silently falls back to normal. Should invalid be a 400? Consistent with difficultyOf rationale (missing should run); for an explicitly wrong value like 'wild' it's better to be lenient — but a typo silently becoming normal is a bit sloppy. Given the whole design says "认不出回落 normal", I'll keep lenient and note it in a comment (and the UI only sends three values). Actually being strict when the field is present but wrong would be better UX... but then old scripts fine. Hmm: `if (body['difficulty'] !== undefined && !isDifficulty(body['difficulty'])) return 400`. That's more honest and cheap. But it contradicts difficultyOf's rationale? No — difficultyOf is for tolerant reads (session.meta), whereas the API can be strict on write. Let me do strict-on-write: clean and testable. I'll add a test for it later.

Let me implement:

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Refactor seedDemoCassettes to loop over three difficulties. Important subtlety: `createDemoScriptedClient(dir)` accumulates entries across runs; the scripted client writes cassettes keyed by prompt digest. Since the digest includes budget numbers in the system prompt, each difficulty produces distinct digests for probe (and answer? answer prompt includes contract? answer prompt likely includes the probe question only; verdict prompt may include... The probe system prompt contains budget → probe cassettes differ per difficulty. Answer/verdict/suggest prompts probably don't include the contract, so their digests likely collide across difficulties (same digest) → same cassette reused. That's fine.

But there's a hazard: the scripted-demo client is *prompt-driven*; if the same digest appears twice, it would just overwrite with identical content. Fine.

Another hazard: a hard-difficulty run may ask more probes (up to 4 rounds) and the scripted demo generates a fixed number of probes per prompt based on the claims in the prompt — scripted probeText probably generates 2 probes per interviewer per call. With 4 rounds, it will call probe 4 times per interviewer → more entries, needing generated text each time. Does scripted-demo produce deterministic content that works for any round? probeText parses the prompt for claims and previous rounds; it should produce questions again (likely the same question each round → dedup gate might reject repeated questions...). The dedup gate in probe.ts: "全局提问去重" — identical question already asked gets rejected. Then probes would be fewer, not a failure. But a failed quality aspect... the demo panels would just have fewer probes. Also, `probeableClaims` requires budget. It'll work, just maybe fewer probes.

Risk: round 4 with no new probes → run.ts pushes notice and breaks. Fine.

Also `budgetSnapshot` etc. Fine.

Another concern: seed time (3 records + 3 replays). Each record run is deterministic scripted (no network), so fast.

Let me restructure:

```ts
export interface DemoDifficultyRun {
  difficulty: Difficulty;
  record: DemoRunSummary;
  replay: DemoRunSummary;
  ok: boolean;
  problems: string[];
  signature: string;
}

export interface DemoSeedReport {
  cassetteDir: string;
  entries: DemoSeedEntry[];
  /** 三档各跑一遍：常规档的字段仍然放在顶层，兼容既有消费者 */
  runs: DemoDifficultyRun[];
  record: DemoRunSummary;
  replay: DemoRunSummary;
  ok: boolean;
  problems: string[];
  pruned: string[];
  signature: string;
}
```

newSessionId(db, mode, difficulty): budget: { config: budgetOf(difficulty), ... }, meta: { source: 'demo-seed', difficulty }.

Then:

```ts
const scripted = createDemoScriptedClient(opts.dir);
const runs: DemoDifficultyRun[] = [];
for (const difficulty of DIFFICULTIES) {
  const recordDb = openDatabase({ path: ':memory:' });
  let recordRun: RunSessionResult;
  try { recordRun = await runAndPersist({ db: recordDb, sessionId: newSessionId(recordDb, 'record', difficulty), client: scripted.client, model: 'scripted-demo' }); }
  finally { closeDatabase(recordDb); }
  const replayDb = openDatabase({ path: ':memory:' });
  let replayRun: RunSessionResult;
  try { replayRun = await runAndPersist({ db: replayDb, sessionId: newSessionId(replayDb, 'replay', difficulty), client: createReplayClient(opts.dir), model: 'fixture' }); }
  finally { closeDatabase(replayDb); }
  const problems = problemsOf(recordRun, replayRun, scripted.entries);
  runs.push({ difficulty, record: summarize(recordRun), replay: summarize(replayRun), ok: problems.length === 0, problems, signature: signatureOf(recordRun) });
}
const pruned = pruneStaleCassettes(...) // after all three records
```

Careful: pruning after all three records — must be after every record run, else the third record would need cassettes already on disk? No: record writes cassettes as it goes; pruning only deletes DEMO_MODEL cassettes whose digest isn't in the final `scripted.entries` set. If we prune after all three, all new digests are present. Correct.

But careful: the entries list accumulates; a digest from gentle is in entries, so kept. Good.

The `problems` checks: `scripted.entries.length === 0`, `required` coverage, signature mismatch, rejectedClaims === 0. The required-stage coverage and entries.length are global (not per-difficulty) — keep them as a global check in `problems` too, plus per-run signature check. Let me structure:

```ts
function runProblems(recordRun, replayRun, difficulty): string[] {
  const problems: string[] = [];
  if (signatureOf(recordRun) !== signatureOf(replayRun)) problems.push(difficulty + ' 档的回放结果与合成结果不一致：…');
  if (replayRun.rejectedClaims.length === 0) problems.push(difficulty + ' 档的演示里没有被拦下的主张：锚定闸门失去了可见的正面证据。');
  return problems;
}
```

Global problems: entries empty → message; missing required stages → message; plus maybe a check that each difficulty produced ≥1 probe? Let's add: if (replayRun.probes.length === 0) problems.push(label + ' 档一条追问都没有…'). Good extra guard.

Top-level: `problems = [...global, ...runs.flatMap(r => r.problems)]`, `ok = problems.length === 0`, `record`/`replay`/`signature` = normal run's (find by difficulty === DEFAULT_DIFFICULTY).

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Interesting! The difficulty ordering? Note the output: 温和 got 10 probes, 常规 and 挑刺 got 0 probes. Why?

Because the scripted-demo client is stateful across runs? It probably keeps internal state per session? Hmm. Actually: the probe cassettes are addressed by prompt digest. Probe prompt includes the claim list AND the contract... wait, does the probe prompt include the budget numbers? The probe *user* prompt may not include budget. The probe *system* prompt is the rendered contract → includes budget → digest differs per difficulty. So each difficulty gets its own probe cassettes. 12 probe cassettes = 3 difficulties × 4 (3 interviewers + ...?). Hmm 12.

But the second/third runs produced 0 probes while the first produced 10. Why would the scripted client produce no probes for later runs?

Likely cause: the "全局提问去重" gate is per-session, so not it. More likely the scripted client (scripted-demo) has a `respond` function that tracks which probes were already asked *within the client instance*, and since we reuse the same `scripted.client` across the three record runs, it thinks everything has been asked already (dedup state in the client, not the session). That's a real finding: the demo scripted client must be created per run, or its dedup memory is keyed by session.

Also note the run order: DIFFICULTIES = gentle, normal, hard; the first (gentle) worked, so the state was fresh.

Also: even in gentle the run produced 2 rounds and 10 probes, but hard produced 0 — consistent with "state accumulates".

Fix: create a fresh scripted client per difficulty, but then `entries` accumulation matters: each client records entries into its own list? createDemoScriptedClient(dir) returns {client, entries} where entries is presumably a live array. If I create one per difficulty, I need to concatenate entries. But there's a subtlety: the cassette writing is keyed by digest; separate clients each write their own entries. Concatenating gives the full list. But if two difficulties produce the same digest (answer/verdict/suggest), the second client would write the same file again → duplicate entries in the combined list (same digest twice). The prune step uses a Set so fine; but `purposeLine` counts would double-count duplicates. Better: dedupe entries by digest when combining.

Alternative cleaner fix: make the scripted client's dedup state resettable/per-session. But the client is a demo fixture; the fact that it holds cross-run state is a latent trap. The honest fix: create a fresh scripted client per difficulty and merge entries with dedupe by digest (keeping the first occurrence's purpose). Let me check scripted-demo.ts to see what state it keeps and whether respond uses a session-scoped structure.

Let me look at the client creation and the probeText dedup.

</details>

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Confirmed: `ScriptState = { asked: Set<string> }` is per-client, and probeText uses `state.asked` to avoid repeating questions across calls within a session — but it lives on the client instance, so reusing one client across three sessions breaks runs 2 and 3.

Options:
A. Create a fresh scripted client per difficulty (in seedDemoCassettes) and merge entries (dedupe by digest).
B. Make the state per-session. But the client has no session identity in LlmRequest (channel maybe). Not clean.

Option A is right and is itself the lesson: 演示脚本的「问过了」记忆挂在客户端实例上，复用它跑第二场就一条都不问了。That's finding-worthy (F58/F59): 把「本次会话的状态」放在客户端实例上，等于偷偷假设「一个客户端只服务一场会话」。

Implementation in demo-seed.ts:

```ts
  const runs: DemoDifficultyRun[] = [];
  const merged: DemoSeedEntry[] = [];
  const seen = new Set<string>();
  for (const difficulty of DIFFICULTIES) {
    // 每一档都要一个**新的**脚本客户端：脚本的「这条问过了」记忆挂在实例上，
    // 复用一个实例跑第二场，第二场会一条都不问（实测温和档 10 条、后两档 0 条）。
    const scripted = createDemoScriptedClient(opts.dir);
    const one = await runOneDifficulty(opts.dir, scripted, difficulty);
    runs.push(one.run);
    for (const entry of scripted.entries) {
      if (seen.has(entry.digest)) continue;
      seen.add(entry.digest);
      merged.push(entry);
    }
  }
  const entries = merged;
  const pruned = pruneStaleCassettes(opts.dir, seen);
```
then replace references `scripted.entries` with `entries`.

For hard: maxRounds 4, perInterviewer 2 → but global maxTotalProbes 80; per round, probe allows MAX_PROBES_PER_TURN=2 per interviewer call... With 1 interviewer and 1 claim: each round 1 question → 4 rounds, 4 probes. Wait, perInterviewerPerClaim=2 means the interviewer can ask at most 2 questions per claim total → after round 2 they're exhausted. Hmm: canProbe checks (a) rounds < maxRoundsPerClaim, (b) probes per interviewer per claim < maxProbesPerInterviewerPerClaim. With 1 claim and 1 interviewer: gentle → per-interviewer limit 1 → 1 probe total, rounds 2 (second round produces no new probes → break with notice). normal → limit 2 → 2 probes, rounds 2 (round3 no new). hard → limit 2 → 2 probes, rounds 2? Because each round asks only 1 question (one interviewer per claim? no — MAX_PROBES_PER_TURN=2 allows an interviewer to ask up to 2 probes in a single call).

Actually probe.ts: per interviewer per open claim, up to 2 probes per call. The scripted client returns propsals; I control how many probes it returns per call. If my script returns 1 probe per call, then normal: round1 1 probe, round2 1 probe → limit 2 reached → rounds 2. hard: same → 2 probes, rounds 2. So to see hard differ, script should return 2 probes per call: then round1 → 2 probes (limit reached for normal), gentle: round1 returns 2 probes but per-interviewer limit 1 → only 1 accepted? canProbe is checked per draft presumably, so second draft rejected by budget gate → 1 probe. Hmm.

Simplest reliable difference: maxRoundsPerClaim. Use 2 interviewers (panel=tech,hr) with different questions, 1 probe each per call. Then per claim per round: 2 probes. 
- gentle: limit per interviewer per claim = 1 → each interviewer 1 probe → total 2, rounds 2 (round 2 → no new probes → notice/break).
- normal: limit 2 → each interviewer 2 → total 4, rounds 2 (round2 gives 2 more, round3 nothing).
- hard: maxRounds 4, limit 2 → total 4, rounds 2? Round 1: 2 probes, round 2: 2 probes (each interviewer at limit 2) → round 3: no probes → break. So hard == normal with 1 claim. Need more claims or more rounds.

Alternative approach: use 3 claims so rounds differ through maxRoundsPerClaim. Hmm, getting complicated. Maybe better to test the budget gate directly at the orchestrator level (there may already be such tests in budget-related test files) — and at HTTP level just assert that the difficulty flows into session budget +契约 and that gentle truly stops earlier.

Practical approach: script returns a *fresh* question for each probe call, per interviewer; with 1 claim and panel=tech,hr:
round 1: tech asks Q1, hr asks Q2 → 2 probes.
round 2: tech asks Q3 (tech count now 2 = limit for normal/hard), hr asks Q4.
- gentle: limit 1 → round 2: tech already at 1 → rejected; hr at 1 → rejected → 0 new probes → break → probes 2, rounds 2 (round 2 happened but no probes → rounds counts? run.ts: rounds increments per loop iteration; if no new probes → break; rounds would be 2). Hmm rounds equal.
- normal: round3: limits reached → 0 probes → break. probes 4, rounds 3? Let's see: round1 probes 2, round2 probes 2, round3 probes 0 → break → rounds = 3.
- hard: same as normal (per-interviewer limit 2, rounds limit 4) → probes 4, rounds 3.
So probes differ: gentle 2 vs normal 4. That's a good assertion. hard differs from normal only via maxRoundsPerClaim (4) and maxTotalProbes (80) — not observable with 1 claim. To observe hard, use 3 claims: then normal's global? no, maxTotalProbes 60 not hit. maxRoundsPerClaim matters only if per-interviewer limits not exhausted... they are (2 per claim). So hard vs normal is hard to distinguish through probes with the same script; that's fine — assert hard's *budget numbers* land in session/contracts, and assert gentle vs normal differ in实际 probes.

```ts
/**
 * 三档追问强度（gentle / normal / hard）：同一套编排，三种预算。

 * 为什么单独一个文件测它：
```

Probe scripting: purpose 'extract' → EXTRACT with 1 claim; 'probe' → return one probe for the requesting interviewer with a unique question. The scripted client's request has channel? In http.test.ts the scripted returns the same probe each time. For counting probes I rely on distinct questions? Dedup: probe.ts has 全局提问去重 — if the same question text repeats, subsequent proposals get dropped as duplicates! That's crucial: I must generate unique question text per call. Use a counter and the channel to vary text.

So:
```ts
function scripted(): ScriptedClient {
  let n = 0;
  return createScriptedClient((req) => {
    if (req.purpose === 'extract') return EXTRACT;
    if (req.purpose === 'probe') {
      n += 1;
      return JSON.stringify({ probes: [{ claimId: 'C1', question: '第 ' + n + ' 次追问：这个数字怎么测的？', quote: QUOTE, rationale: '追问测量方法' }] });
    }
    if (req.purpose === 'answer') return ANSWER;
    if (req.purpose === 'verdict') return VERDICT;
    if (req.purpose === 'suggest') return JSON.stringify({ suggestions: [] });
    return new Error('脚本化模型没有为 purpose=' + req.purpose + ' 准备返回值');
  });
}
```

gentle: per-interviewer limit 1 → round1: tech 1 (count 1), hr 1 (count 1) → 2 probes. round2: both rejected → 0 → break. probes 2, rounds 2.
normal: round1 2 probes, round2 2 probes (each count 2 = limit) → 4 probes; round3 → 0 → break; rounds 3.
So assert gentle probes 2, normal probes 4.

Good. Now write the test file. Careful about quoting. I'll build lines as JS single-quoted strings with \' escapes where TS uses single quotes, and no double quotes anywhere.

Actually TS code needs single quotes for strings; inside my JS single-quoted line literals I must escape them: '...\'...\''. That's error-prone but manageable. Alternative: use JS double-quoted lines for JS and escape nothing? My program body is TS-ish JS; I can use double-quoted JS strings — but the toolchain lesson says run_code program body strings can't contain double quotes (the parser chokes). So use single-quoted JS strings with \' escapes inside.

Hmm, another approach: write the file content using JSON via `JSON.stringify`? No.

Safer approach: write the content with a placeholder: use @@ for every single quote in TS source, then .split('@@').join(String.fromCharCode(39)). That way my JS lines contain no quotes at all (use single-quoted JS strings but avoid any quote chars inside). 

So each line: 'import { DEFAULT_BUDGET } from @@crossexam/shared@@;' then replace @@ with '.

Also for type annotations etc. no quotes. For strings inside TS like '脚本化模型没有为 purpose=' + req.purpose + ' 准备返回值' → '脚本化模型没有为 purpose=@@ + req.purpose + @@ 准备返回值'.

```ts
  });
}
```

Now A1: 让求职者补一句真话 (supplement). Design from P10 review:
- 新增 supplement 表随会话（schema v8）
- 报告里简历证据与补充证据分栏
- 不回写简历原文

Let me revisit what A1 was: "A1 让求职者补一句真话 ★★★★★" — when a claim is judged 证据缺失 (unsupported), the user can supply a supplementary statement (a real fact they didn't put on the resume), and the interviewer re-asks/re-evaluates? Design needed.

Given the compressed summary: "A1 让求职者补一句真话（新增 supplement 表随会话，报告里简历证据与补充证据分栏，不回写简历原文）".

So the feature: after a session, for claims that need evidence, the user can add a supplement sentence. Effects:
1. Stored per session (supplement table).
2. The supplement is fed into... a re-run? Maybe into the answer/verdict stage on a rerun? Or shown in the report as separate evidence column, and optionally the suggestion generation uses it?

Let me design concretely and keep it small and defensible:
- API: POST /api/sessions/:id/supplements {claimId, text} → stores.
- GET returns supplements in SessionView.
- UI: in the ClaimBoard, each claim with verdict needing evidence gets a "补一句真话" input; saved to server.
- Effect: a supplement is not resume text — it's evidence the candidate supplies. It should influence verdict? That requires LLM re-judging → extra cassette stage (cost). Alternative cheaper design: supplements are included in the report (「简历证据 vs 补充证据」) and can be adopted into the revised resume (as a new line) when the user confirms — i.e., integrate with revision flow: supplement text can be applied as an addition, not a rewrite.

Hmm, "让求职者补一句真话" — the intent is: the resume lacks evidence for a claim; the candidate can type a real fact; the tool then (a) shows it as supplementary evidence in the report, and (b) uses it to produce a concrete rewrite suggestion "补进简历的一句话".

The A1 priority in the review: 让求职者补一句真话. And A4 is 反问环节 (counter-question stage).

Given remaining effort and the risk of scope creep, the smallest valuable A1:
1. schema v8: `supplement` table (id, session_id, claim_id, text, created_at) + repo functions.
2. HTTP: PUT/POST supplements (validate via zod: 4–300 chars, must not be empty), DELETE, and GET included in SessionView; plus a new pure function in shared: `supplementNote`/`appliedSupplements`?
3. Report: 「补充证据」 section listing claim + supplement, and criteria 「简历里没写 vs 求职者补充」分栏.
4. Revision flow: allow adopting the supplement as an *addition* line to the resume — the accept API currently only takes claimIds and applies suggestions. To keep ADR-0009's collection semantics, I could add supplements as another input: acceptRevisions(db, sessionId, claimIds, supplementalClaimIds?) hmm.

Simplify: supplement is text; when adopting, treat it as an appended paragraph candidate named 「补充说明」? That mixes semantics.

Alternative cleaner design: the supplement is *evidence*, and it feeds the **suggestion** stage: with a supplement present for claim X, re-running the suggest stage can produce a rewrite that includes the supplement. But re-running suggest only for some claims = new cassette prompts and demo re-record.

Cheapest honest feature that satisfies "让求职者补一句真话":
- It is stored with the session and merged into the **revised resume** as an addition under a clearly marked section (e.g., a line appended at the end: 「补充说明：…」)? Hmm, that changes the resume.

Let me reconsider: maybe A1 was intended as: the interviewed candidate (求职者 Agent) currently only answers from the resume; where the interviewer finds no evidence, the *user* (human candidate) can supply a real fact so the system can (1) record it as additional evidence and (2) rewrite the resume to include it. That's what "补一句真话" means: 补 = 补进简历。So the acceptance path should produce a resume line.

Design decision (D): supplements are **addition-only revisions** — the revision engine already supports revisions with before='' / after=text? Let me check shared/revise.ts: Revision has {claimId, before, after, why, ...}? If `before` is empty string, applyRevisions might treat as append at end. <details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

The revision engine supports only replacements on existing ranges (start<end, sameText). A supplement is new text not in the resume → not applicable. So a separate "补一句" mechanism.

A1 design (final, keep tight):

Concept: **补充证据 (supplement)** — the candidate types a real fact for a claim that came back 证据缺失/无法回答. The system:
1. Records it on the session (schema v8, table `supplement`: id, session_id, claim_id, text, created_at).
2. Shows it in the interface next to the claim ("你补的这句：…") and in the report as a separate evidence column: 「简历里写了什么 / 你补了什么」.
3. **Feeds it back into the沙盘**: re-running the session uses the supplements as extra evidence for the answer stage. Hmm — that means the 求职者 Agent answers with the supplement. That's the real value: "补一句真话" then the interviewer asks again and the verdict can become 站得住.

Implementation choice: add supplements to the *resume text* used by a re-run? If a supplement is appended to the resume text for re-run, the whole prompt changes → all cassettes invalid unless re-recorded for a "with supplement" variant. Costly but honest.

Cheaper: supplements go into the **answer** stage prompt as "候选人对这些主张的补充说明" and into the **verdict** stage? Also changes prompts → cassettes for those stages invalid for sessions with supplements. Since demo has no supplements, demo cassettes stay valid. Live mode would just work. For replay mode with supplements → cassette missing (acceptable: supplements are a live/re-run feature; and record mode can capture).

Hmm, but then offline demo can't demo A1. That's a demo-coverage problem (F56: offline demo coverage = demo script coverage). To demo A1 offline, I could record a demo variant with supplements... that doubles cassettes.

```
export function insertionPointAfter(resumeText, anchor): number {
  const from = Math.max(0, Math.min(anchor.end, resumeText.length));
  const newline = resumeText.indexOf('\n', from);
  return newline < 0 ? resumeText.length : newline;
}
```

```text
export function supplementRevision(
  claimId: string,
  resumeText: string,
  anchor: Anchor,
  text: string,
): { claimId: string; start: number; end: number; before: string; after: string } {
  const at = insertionPointAfter(resumeText, anchor);
  return { claimId, start: at, end: at, before: '', after: supplementBlock(text) };
}
```

Now modify revise.ts to support insertions:

Line 106 check: `end <= start` rejects insertions. Change to allow `end === start` when `item.before.length === 0 && item.after.length > 0` (insertion). Otherwise require end > start.

Line 120: sameText check for insertion: slice(start, end) is '' and before is '' → sameText('', '') returns true (actual===claimed). Good already.

Line 129: applied.before = resumeText.slice(start,end) = '' for insertion. Good.

Line 114 `start < lastEnd` overlap guard: for insertion at the same position as a replacement's start... e.g., a replacement [10,20) and an insertion at 30 → fine. Insertion at 20 (end of replacement) → start 20 >= lastEnd 20 → allowed. Good, they can coexist as designed.

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now add SUPPLEMENT_TABLE and schema v8 migration.

Design of the table:
```
const SUPPLEMENT_TABLE =
  'CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS supplement (' +
  '  session_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES session(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,' +
  '  claim_id TEXT NOT NULL,' +
  '  text TEXT NOT NULL,' +
  '  digest TEXT NOT NULL,' +
  '  start INTEGER NOT NULL,' +
  '  end INTEGER NOT NULL,' +
  '  created_at TEXT NOT NULL,' +
  '  PRIMARY KEY (session_id, claim_id)' +
  ')';
```
Storing start/end (the insertion point computed at save time, relative to the original resume) means the export can reuse it directly without recomputation — and it's frozen evidence of where it went. But careful: if computed at save time from original resume, and resume never changes, it's stable. Good. But should we recompute at accept time? Better: store the user's text and compute insertion point at accept time from the claim anchor (code-computed). If we store start/end at save time we duplicate. Hmm — storing them risks staleness if the claim anchor changed (it can't; claims are frozen per session). Storing them also lets us show "插在第 N 行后".

Decision: store only text + digest (evidence of what the user typed), compute the insertion point at accept time from the claim's anchor (single source of truth = the anchor). Simpler and can't go stale. I'll note this in the migration comment.

So:
```
const SUPPLEMENT_TABLE =
  'CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS supplement (' +
  '  session_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES session(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,' +
  '  claim_id TEXT NOT NULL,' +
  '  text TEXT NOT NULL,' +
  '  digest TEXT NOT NULL,' +
  '  created_at TEXT NOT NULL,' +
  '  updated_at TEXT NOT NULL,' +
  '  PRIMARY KEY (session_id, claim_id)' +
  ')';
```
Migration v8 = rebuild with just this table.

Then repo.ts: SupplementRow { sessionId, claimId, text, digest, createdAt, updatedAt }, saveSupplement(db, {sessionId, claimId, text, at?}), listSupplements(db, sessionId), getSupplement(db, sessionId, claimId), deleteSupplement(db, sessionId, claimId): boolean, clearSupplements(db, sessionId)?

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now write the changes. Plan for acceptRevisions:

```ts
  const supplements = new Map(listSupplements(db, sessionId).map((row) => [row.claimId, row]));
  const candidates: Revision[] = [];
  const early: RevisionSkip[] = [];
  for (const id of wanted) {
    const claim = byId.get(id);
    if (claim === undefined) { early.push({ claimId: id, reason: '这条主张不在这份简历里' }); continue; }
    const supplement = supplements.get(id);
    if (claim.suggestion === null && supplement === undefined) {
      early.push({ claimId: id, reason: '这条主张既没有改写建议，也没有补充说明' });
      continue;
    }
    if (claim.suggestion !== null) candidates.push(revisionOf(id, claim.suggestion));
    if (supplement !== undefined) candidates.push(supplementRevision(id, session.resumeText, claim.anchor, supplement.text));
  }
```

```
const REVISION_TABLE =
  'CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS revision (' +
  '  session_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES session(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,' +
  '  claim_id TEXT NOT NULL,' +
  '  ordinal INTEGER NOT NULL,' +
```

```
/**

 *
 * v8 改成 (session_id, ordinal)，因为一条主张现在可能同时落下两条改动：
 * 一条替换型（改写建议）+ 一条插入型（补充说明）。旧主键会让第二条把第一条覆盖掉。
 * 迁移必须从**当时**的形态出发，所以这个形状要冻结在这里，不能跟着 REVISION_TABLE 走。
 */
const REVISION_TABLE_V5 = REVISION_TABLE.replace('PRIMARY KEY (session_id, ordinal)', 'PRIMARY KEY (session_id, claim_id)');

const REVISION_INDEX = 'CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS revision_by_claim ON revision(session_id, claim_id)';
```

migrateV8 content:
```ts
/**
 * v7 → v8：补充说明落库，并让一条主张能落下两条改动。
 *
 * 两件事一起做，因为它们是一件事的两面：
 *   - 新增 supplement 表：用户当场补的那句真话（它是新事实，简历上没有原句可改）；
 *   - 重建 revision 表：主键从 (session_id, claim_id) 换成 (session_id, ordinal)，
 *     否则同一条主张的「替换 + 插入」两条改动会互相覆盖（ON CONFLICT 只留后一条）。
 *
 * 老库搬过来的既有改写一条都不丢：列没变，只是主键换了。
 */
function migrateV8(db: Db): void {
  rebuild(
    db,
    () => {
      db.exec('ALTER TABLE revision RENAME TO revision_v5');
      db.exec(REVISION_TABLE);
      db.exec(REVISION_INDEX);
      db.exec(
        'INSERT INTO revision (session_id, claim_id, ordinal, start, end, before, after, created_at)' +
          ' SELECT session_id, claim_id, ordinal, start, end, before, after, created_at FROM revision_v5',
      );
      db.exec('DROP TABLE revision_v5');
      db.exec(SUPPLEMENT_TABLE);
    },
    8,
  );
}
```
Wait: ALTER TABLE revision RENAME TO revision_v5 — if a legacy DB is at v5/v6/v7, revision exists with old shape. Fine. But if someone's DB somehow lacks the revision table... not possible past v5.

Careful: rename keeps index revision_by_claim attached to revision_v5? Indexes follow the renamed table; dropping revision_v5 drops it. But at v7 there's no revision_by_claim index (it's new in v8), so nothing to drop. Fine.

Also with rebuild()'s PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF outside transaction... fine.

```ts
  /**
   * 记一句补充说明：面试官说「简历里没写」时，用户当场补一句真话。
   * 它不是改写——原文没有原句可改——所以走单独的表，采纳时作为插入型改动落在行尾。
   */
  app.post('/api/sessions/:id/supplements', async (c) => {
    const id = c.req.param('id');
    const body = await readBody(c);
    const claimId = textField(body['claimId']);
    if (claimId === '') return c.json({ error: '要指明这是哪一条主张的补充说明。' }, 400);
    const result = saveSupplementFor(db, id, claimId, textField(body['text']));
    if (!result.ok) return c.json({ error: result.error }, result.code);
    return c.json({ supplement: result.row }, 201);
  });

  /** 删掉一条补充说明：改主意是正常的，改完的简历下次确认就会少一行 */
  app.delete('/api/sessions/:id/supplements/:claimId', (c) => {
    const id = c.req.param('id');
    const claimId = c.req.param('claimId');
    if (getSession(db, id) === null) return c.json({ error: '会话不存在：' + id }, 404);
    const removed = removeSupplementFor(db, id, claimId);
    if (!removed) return c.json({ error: '这条主张还没有补充说明：' + claimId }, 404);
    return c.json({ ok: true, removed });
  });
```
